Codex art prompts

Start here

Launcher prompt: paste this into Codex

What it does. One conversation: Codex fetches branch claude/codex-queue, reads codex-queue/README.md and queue.json from it, and runs every ready group as a parallel sub-agent, each in its own worktree on G:, then reports the summary. The same text is in the repository as codex-queue/LAUNCH.md.

The cards below. The per-group cards below are the same prompts, kept for reference and for manual runs. The queue's own copies (codex-queue/prompts/ on that branch) differ only in the branch and worktree instructions at their start.

You are the orchestrator of the Terepasztal art queue. G:/DEV/Terepasztal/terepasztal is read-only for you (it has uncommitted work): never edit or switch anything there, and never write to C:.
1. git -C G:/DEV/Terepasztal/terepasztal fetch -q origin claude/codex-queue
2. Make the ledger worktree: git -C G:/DEV/Terepasztal/terepasztal worktree add -b claude/codex-queue G:/DEV/wt/codex-queue origin/claude/codex-queue (if it already exists: fetch there and git merge --ff-only origin/claude/codex-queue).
3. Read G:/DEV/wt/codex-queue/codex-queue/README.md and queue.json in full, and follow them.
4. Run every group with status "ready" as its own sub-agent, in parallel, each in its own git worktree on G: (never in the main checkout); the prompts of a group run in order. If you cannot run sub-agents in parallel, run the groups one by one as the README says.
5. Honour every stop condition in the prompts: a stop halts only that group. Never force-push, never delete branches.
6. After each prompt the sub-agent appends its result to codex-queue/status/<group>.md and pushes it (see the README, "Ledger").
7. When all groups are done or stopped, write and push codex-queue/status/SUMMARY.md and report the summary to me here.

Updated 2026-10-20.

Each group below is ONE Codex conversation: paste its prompts one after another, each after the previous one finishes. The button Copy whole conversation copies the group's prompts in order, with a separator line between them; Copy prompt copies a single one.

Codex works in the main checkout (G:\DEV\Terepasztal\terepasztal), which has one branch checked out at a time. So run ONE group at a time there. Two groups can run at the same time only if Codex gives each its own git worktree. Every group is on its own branch, except that the Later prompt C33b belongs to the Rotations conversation (same branch, art/535-rotations).

Turned views are painted over Core's 3D scaffolds (branch claude/art-scaffolds-review-2, head fc07d589); each prompt says how to read them. Accepted pictures are not painted again.

C18a

Big Station: r1, r3 and r2 painted over the scaffolds (3 pictures)

1 of 14

Status. Ready. r0 stays; nothing painted yet.

Paint 3 turned views of the Big Station (the old station picture) for the isometric railway game in this repository, for issue #535 (review 2 of the building pictures): r1, then r3, then r2. r0 is the old picture, assets/source/base-v1/station.png: it stays as it is and is not painted again. The footprint is 2 x 1 tiles at r0 (the review's footprint; the game data still says 1 x 1). Each view is painted OVER a 3D scaffold render of that view (Core made it from r0 at the game's exact camera): the render fixes the shape, the camera and the layout, and you repaint every surface of it in the game's painted style, matching r0's materials and details and painting the surfaces r0 never showed anew. A picture is accepted when its silhouette matches the scaffold's outline (overlap at least 0.90, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px); the camera comes from the scaffold, so there is no separate camera test.

Work on branch art/535-rotations. It is the lane of the turned views of the old pictures and may not exist yet: git fetch origin develop art/535-rotations. If origin/art/535-rotations exists: git switch art/535-rotations (git switch -c art/535-rotations origin/art/535-rotations if it is not local yet), git pull --ff-only, then git merge origin/develop once if the branch is behind it (stop and say where if it conflicts). If it does not exist: git switch -c art/535-rotations origin/develop, and push it with git push -u origin art/535-rotations at your first commit. Touch nothing outside assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/ (make it, with raw/ inside) and your own measuring script assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/measure-big_station.mjs (below). Never push to main or develop. Commit subjects read "Art: <outcome>". Keep every picture your image tool returns, byte for byte, as raw/big_station-r<v>-attempt-<k>.png (v the view, k from 1 for each view; if the job's prompts.json already holds attempts of a view, go on from its highest k and never overwrite or attach one), and record each call in assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/prompts.json: {view, attempt, file, prompt, references (in order), returned (the size it came back), measured (the numbers below), kept}. Never delete or overwrite a picture. Never commit the scaffolds (below). Ask the tool for 1024 x 1024 and record the size it returns (a 1024 px wide canvas has come back 1254 px wide; 1536 x 1024 came back as asked).

SHARED BRANCH. Several Codex windows work on art/535-rotations at the same time, each in its own folder under assets/source/rotations-535/. Touch only yours. Before every push: git fetch origin art/535-rotations, then git rebase origin/art/535-rotations (their commits touch other folders), then push; if a push is refused, fetch, rebase and push again. Never force-push.

STATE OF THE FOLDER. ACCEPTED: none. Nothing is painted under this job yet. A window read r0 on 19 October and painted nothing. If assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/ is on the branch with raw pictures, keep them, go on from their attempt numbers and never attach them; otherwise start clean. r0 is read, not gated.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE SCAFFOLDS. For every view you paint there is a 3D scaffold render: Core rebuilt the building as a 3D model from its painted r0 and rendered it at the game's exact camera on the guide's canvas. The scaffolds are on a branch that is not merged yet (art: 3D scaffold renders for the turned-view painting jobs, review 2), and they are not to be committed to your art branch. From the repository root:

  git fetch origin claude/art-scaffolds-review-2

(its head is fc07d589 or later). Make a folder OUTSIDE the repository, say ../scaffolds-535, and write out of that branch, byte for byte (a PowerShell redirect corrupts a PNG: use bash, or cmd /c, or git cat-file blob with --filters off), these 9 files:

  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r1-lit.png > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r1-lit.png
  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r1-bare.png > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r1-bare.png
  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r1.json > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r1.json
  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r3-lit.png > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r3-lit.png
  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r3-bare.png > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r3-bare.png

  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r2-bare.png > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r2-bare.png
  git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/big_station-r2.json > ../scaffolds-535/big_station-r2.json

Per view, in assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/big_station/ on that branch: big_station-r<v>-lit.png is the render with the painting projected back onto the model, its light corrected for the turn: it is the picture you PAINT OVER (shape, camera, layout). big_station-r<v>-bare.png is the same shape in plain clay on a white background, and big_station-r<v>.json holds outline_polygon_px (the silhouette, canvas px), the base and footprint corners, the camera numbers, the canvas, the anchor and the scaffold's own verdict: they are for the SILHOUETTE CHECK, never attached to a call. Read assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds/README.md on that branch the same way (how the scaffolds were made, what each is good for). Check that every lit and bare picture is 1024 x 1024 and that each json names job "big_station" and its view and canvas [1024, 1024]; if not, stop and say so. Never git add anything from ../scaffolds-535. Read assets/source/buildings-v2/GUIDE.md too (on develop, or from the branch claude/art-guides-review-2 with git show, written out beside the scaffolds): its section 2 (the rules every picture follows) and sections 5 to 7 (making one picture, looking at it, when it fails). The grey plan guides of that branch are not used by this job any more: the scaffolds replace them. The building queue does not handle this job: do not run tools/building-queue.mjs.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE MASTER (r0). Master: assets/source/base-v1/station.png, sha256 df48b186fa1e622613c99b6b3fd9473c055626e9208f65f2033c3665d02f49c3. It is the building as the game shows it today, and r0 is finished: it stays as it is and is not painted again, not edited, not moved. Check its hash first; if it differs, stop and say so. It has a dark glow round the building: that glow is not in the picture you are to make; paint a transparent background. What r0 shows, to carry round the building: a long, low station building of cream limestone courses with a dark slate gable roof whose ridge runs along the long front, three cream-stone chimney stacks on the ridge with two red clay pots each. The FRONT (the long lower-left wall) is the platform side: a deep cream corrugated canopy with a scalloped fascia on dark-green cast-iron posts with curved brackets on stone footings, hanging lanterns, two cast-iron benches with timber seats, a forest-green plank door with a brass knob and two small windows with cream surrounds. The right-hand end (the lower-right wall) is a quoined gable with scalloped bargeboards, an arched forest-green door with a cream surround, an arched window, one more in the gable, a black downpipe, and a small slate-roofed lean-to annex at its far end with its own green door.

THE VIEWS, one line each: what the scaffold shows and what it invented. Canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px.

r1 (the front faces the upper left, hidden): INVENTED in the render: the long back wall (the lower-right wall). Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (warn): Gable end with doors and windows comes from r0 and is clean. The long back wall is invented: plain stone, soft, a bench and window smeared in.
r3 (the front faces the lower right): INVENTED in the render: the canopy's underside and the rear gable end (the lower-left wall). Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (warn): Platform wall and canopy seen from the other side: canopy underside and posts smeared. The far gable end is invented but plain.
r2 (the front faces the upper right, hidden): INVENTED in the render: both walls (the back and the rear gable end). Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (warn): Both walls here are invented: plain stone, blurred windows, no door. Roof, chimneys and lean-to shape are right.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

THE ORDER. Paint r1, then r3, then r2. Every call is an image EDIT of the lit scaffold render of its view, 1024 x 1024, transparent background: attach it first, then the references in the order below, never more. The bare render and the json are never attached. r2 is painted last, over its own lit scaffold, with the master and the kept r1 and r3 (attach them only if they passed; if one failed, record it and paint r2 from the master and the words alone).

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a rough 3D render, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral isometric railway game: the Big Station, a long cream-stone railway station building with a platform canopy, the SAME building as in the third picture, in the view named under View. Paint over the first picture: its shape, outline, camera and layout stay exactly as they are (every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part where the render has it, nothing added outside its outline or taken out of it), but EVERY surface is painted again in the game's painted style, that of the second picture, the style board: crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The render's surfaces are soft, smeared and patchy, and the walls that the front view never showed were invented by the 3D reconstruction (they are named under View). Paint all of them anew: where the third picture shows a surface, take its stones, bricks, planks, doors, windows and fittings from it, crisp; where the render has invented a surface, paint it as this same building would have it, plausibly and in the same style, from the materials and details of the sides that are known, and never leave it soft, blurred, smeared or plain. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest (the render's light was corrected for the turn; the painting follows the game's light). Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the render's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the render's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with a clear margin all round, as the render does.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it that the render does not have; keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight where the render has it, with no bushes, crates, steps or loose objects added in front of it."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the quoined gable end with scalloped bargeboards, the arched green door, the arched windows, the black downpipe and the small slate-roofed lean-to annex with its green door (the annex stands inside the footprint). The lower-right wall is the BACK: a plain long stone wall under the slate roof, built as r0 shows the building built (cream limestone courses, quoined corners, the same base), with a few small windows and one plain green door, and no canopy, no benches and no lanterns. The roof is r0's: one dark slate gable roof, its ridge running along the long walls, with the three chimney stacks (two red pots each) on the ridge at the same places along it; the canopy is a lean-to on the front only.

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): the long back wall (the lower-right wall). Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long platform front, with its canopy, lanterns, benches, door and windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them."

PART B, r3:

"View. The building is turned three quarter turns clockwise seen from above. The front faces the lower right. The lower-left wall is the left-hand side; the lower-right wall is the FRONT. The lower-right wall is the FRONT and is r0's lower-left wall, turned to the other side and not mirrored: the long platform front: the deep cream corrugated canopy with its scalloped fascia on dark-green cast-iron posts, the hanging lanterns, two cast-iron benches, the forest-green plank door and two windows. The lower-left wall is the left-hand side: a quoined stone gable end with scalloped bargeboards and one arched window, plainer than the right-hand end: no door and no lean-to annex. The roof is r0's: one dark slate gable roof, its ridge running along the long walls, with the three chimney stacks (two red pots each) on the ridge at the same places along it; the canopy is a lean-to on the front only.

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): the canopy's underside and the rear gable end (the lower-left wall). Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again."

PART B, r2:

"View. The building is turned half round. The front faces the upper right, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the BACK, plainer than the front; the lower-right wall is the left-hand side. The lower-left wall is the BACK, the same wall as the lower-right wall of the r1 picture: a plain long stone wall under the slate roof, built as r0 shows the building built (cream limestone courses, quoined corners, the same base), with a few small windows and one plain green door, and no canopy, no benches and no lanterns. The lower-right wall is the left-hand side, the same wall as the lower-left wall of the r3 picture: a quoined stone gable end with scalloped bargeboards and one arched window, plainer than the right-hand end: no door and no lean-to annex. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from these words. The roof is r0's: one dark slate gable roof, its ridge running along the long walls, with the three chimney stacks (two red pots each) on the ridge at the same places along it; the canopy is a lean-to on the front only.

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): both walls (the back and the rear gable end). Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long platform front, with its canopy, lanterns, benches, door and windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/measure-big_station.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, three chimney stacks with two red pots each, the canopy on the front wall only, with its lanterns and benches; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/big_station-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Big Station turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/big_farm/measure-big_farm.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the louvred dormer, the chimney, the round silo with its pipe and spout on the long right-hand wall only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/big_farm/big_farm-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Big Farm Halt turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE MASTER (r0). Master: assets/source/base-v1/townhouse-civic.png, sha256 0724c2d1e484cd824bddde8c674fa1d44b45e68ccd040d4ebaa6b3efd9cd6601. It is the building as the game shows it today, and r0 is finished: it stays as it is and is not painted again, not edited, not moved. Check its hash first; if it differs, stop and say so. What r0 shows, to carry round the building: a two-storey civic hall of warm red brick with cream limestone quoins and string courses and a dark slate gable roof whose ridge runs along the long front. In the middle of the FRONT (the long lower-left wall) stands a pedimented stone portico on columns with a forest-green double door under a fanlight and stone steps; above it a stepped gable with a round white clock face; on the ridge behind it a small bell cupola with a slate dome and a gold finial; two topiary shrubs in cream stone planters and two brass lanterns beside the entrance; arched windows with cream surrounds on both storeys. Two red-brick chimney stacks with two red pots each stand at the two ends of the ridge. The right-hand end (the lower-right wall) is a brick gable end with cream quoins and a stepped parapet, two columns of arched windows on two storeys and a small arched attic window.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with the portico, the green double door, the clock gable, the planters and the windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/town_hall/measure-town_hall.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the bell cupola and gold finial in the middle of the ridge, two chimney stacks at the ends; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/town_hall/town-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Town hall turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

The FRONT (the long lower-left wall) has three forest-green plank sliding doors hung on an iron rail, two pushed open onto timber-framed bays with a stone floor, a small window with a cream surround at the left end and green downpipes. The right-hand end (the lower-right wall) is a brick gable with cream quoins, scalloped bargeboards and an arched window, with a deep lean-to canopy of cream corrugated sheet on forest-green timber posts with stone footings and iron brackets, and a window and a green door under it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with its three green sliding doors, two open. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/warehouse/measure-warehouse.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, three chimney stacks, three green sliding doors on the front only, the corrugated canopy on the right-hand end only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/warehouse/warehouse-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Warehouse turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 464 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 464 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long window wall with the flywheel and the bending pipe. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/pump/measure-pump.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the flywheel and the pipe with the strainer drum on the front (long window) wall only, the door and lamp on the gable; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/pump/pump-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Water pump turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.
