PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 464 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 464 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/grinder/measure-grinder.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the red-brick chimney, the red-brick arched window in the gable, the lean-to on the right-hand end only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/grinder/grinder-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Stone grinder turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 464 px of the canvas (1024 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 464 canvas px times returned width / 1024).

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): the far sides of the pipe runs, the tank ends and the back of the furnace frame. Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this plant as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME plant at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again. The fourth reference is this same plant in view r1 and the fifth in view r3 (the kept pictures, only if they passed): the sides this view shares with them must look here as they look there, copied as they stand (the same height, shape, parts and details); paint the rest from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/refinery/measure-refinery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the distillation column's height: from the base's near corner row to the column's top, over the base's width e.x - w.x (read the column's foot by eye on a crop where r0 and your picture hide it behind other units, and say so). Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the same units (one long white tank, one tall column with three railed platforms, one furnace block with a chimney, the copper vessels and the white sphere), the copper pipework joining the same units; (b) turned, not mirrored, and every part where the View line puts it; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): both long walls. Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long wall of three tall arched windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those references: the same height, shape under the roof, windows and doors, painted in the same way. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/power_plant/measure-power_plant.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1536; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1536 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 15 canvas px (3 game px; 5 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 15 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the two glazed lanterns on the ridge, the tall chimney with its cream bands, the annex with the generator casing and pipes; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1536 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1536).

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/battery/measure-battery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1536; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1536 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 18 canvas px (3 game px; 6 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 18 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the portal frame's height: from the pad's near corner row to the top of its crossbeam, over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same equipment as the old Battery picture and the kept r0: the olive-green finned transformer tank, three brown insulator stacks with their curved cables, the grey lattice portal frame with its crossbeam, the control cabinet with the hazard sign, the pipes and the green side box, all on the pad of cream limestone flags, at the same places (r1 to r3 against the kept r0); (b) turned, not mirrored, and every part where the View line puts it; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r0 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (12 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/battery/substation-r<v>.png (r0, r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Battery repainted, r0, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1536 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1536).

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this plant as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME plant at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/colliery/measure-colliery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1536; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1536 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 18 canvas px (3 game px; 6 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 18 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the headgear tower's height: from the foot of the tower's nearer legs to the top of its winding wheel, over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, one headgear tower with its wheel and cable, a gantry with three coal hoppers, the lift-house, and the brick engine house with its chimney; (b) turned, not mirrored, and every part where the View line puts it; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/colliery/colliery-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Colliery turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 694 px of the canvas (1536 px wide), so a uniform scale distorts nothing and you do not try to hit it: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize, crop, move or stretch a returned picture yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, its outline included (tools/building-fit.mjs measureBase; where it puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values); chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned picture) and k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement (the held width is 694 canvas px times returned width / 1536).

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): the open hall seen from the rear. Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this plant as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME plant at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again."

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/ironworks/measure-ironworks.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1536; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1536 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 18 canvas px (3 game px; 6 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 18 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the chimney's height: from the foot of the furnace to the top of the chimney, over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the furnace with its chimney and its glowing mouth, the forge shed with its teal roof and clerestory, the open store with ingots and the hoist; (b) turned, not mirrored, and every part where the View line puts it; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/ironworks/ironworks-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Ironworks turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the height number against the scaffold's and r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue #536). The earlier rounds painted the views on grey block-outs, and the painter could not hold the game's camera from a block-out: of every picture painted on 19 October, none passed. The author decided that the turned views are now painted OVER 3D scaffolds rebuilt from r0 at the game's exact camera: the camera, the plan and the footprint come with the scaffold, and what is left for the painter is the painting. Paint on the lit render of the view, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painted width is a measurement and Art scales a picture uniformly at packing; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

The beam's pivot, the horse-head and the crank wheel are as tall as in r0; the machine is the same size.

Invented in the first picture (the front view never showed it): the tank, the back of the beam and the lattice. Paint these surfaces anew, crisp and plausible, in this building's own materials and details; the first picture's soft or smeared look is not kept.

References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. The third is this plant as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and character from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; the shape, the angle, the outline and the layout are the first picture's and do not change, and the third picture's angle, footprint and any dark glow round it are not used. Paint the SAME plant at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/oil_derrick/measure-oil_derrick.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the Samson post's height: from the skid frame's top at the post's foot to the beam's pivot, over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the lattice A-frame, the walking beam with its horse-head, the crank wheel and pitman arm, the gearbox, the banded tank, the wellhead, the same copper pipes; (b) turned, not mirrored, and every part where the View line puts it; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (9 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to assets/source/rotations-535/oil_derrick/oil_derrick-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art: Oil derrick turned, r1, r3, r2 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The picture's width no longer refuses a picture. Paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it: it is not a mirror image and not a copy of the third picture, and do not paint the third picture again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with the green door and the two windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from the words above."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/house/measure-house.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your script reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's scaffold json from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the two feet's slopes (rise over run; 0.50 for a 30 degree camera), reported and never refused; the height number below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

SILHOUETTE, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px, 1024 x 1536), filled and scaled by s0 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute: (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture (a uniform scale, then the base placed on the footprint), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (divide pixels of the returned picture by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (the symmetric Hausdorff distance), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px on this canvas). A picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit: the shape comes from the painting over the scaffold.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the scaffold's bare render once for each view (a zoomed crop) the way the height is read below and write the number to prompts.json as scaffoldReference, and read r0 the same way as r0Reference (and its counts of near-white and near-amber pixels).

- silhouette: as above (IoU at least 0.90 at the best fit, boundary deviation at most 24 canvas px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal (THE SIZE). The picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way: within 15 per cent of the scaffold's (scaffoldReference); r0's number goes in the record as r0Reference;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall (the game's light comes from the upper left), by eye on a crop; a machine estimate (the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet) is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the lit scaffold: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof form, colours and details, the cream chimney stack with its two red pots, the corbelled gable edge, the green door and the two windows on the front only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) EVERY SURFACE REPAINTED: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS: each has crisp courses, planks, windows or fittings of the same building, and none is a plain wash; the walls r0 shows carry r0's details, crisp; (d) no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r3 left the chimney's top 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a brick wall is wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what is listed under REFERENCES, never a picture that failed, never a .rejected file. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view (6 calls in all). If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept: false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

Look at each kept picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side with r0: they should be one building of one size.
