THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/hydro_plant/measure-hydro_plant.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours); the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read. Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read nothing: there is no old picture to read for the height; the plan gives the number.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and k = the held width / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio, 1.439 (1.22 to 1.65), and the long side lower left;

- camera, STRICT (this is the picture the scaffolds of stage 2 are built from): both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30), and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Steps, door leaves, pipes, the intake and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the powerhouse wall's height at the near corner s: foot to the eaves, which is the plan's block of 40 game px (320 guide px), 0.82 of the base's width of 390.5 px; there is no old r0 to read for the new design, so the plan's number is the test. MEASURE BEFORE YOU RECORD (after take, before set), in this order. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

- silhouette, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (../scaffolds-535/farm-r<v>.json: canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 = returned width / 1024 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture, chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (symmetric Hausdorff), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs an IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px). Write the small script yourself (it reads pictureOf from tools/building-check.mjs for the alpha; it is not committed unless the folder already has a measuring script for this family); a picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit;

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base is held at 416 px of the canvas (509 of a 1254 px picture): on a crop, read the outer edge of the base's left corner and of its right corner (the foot of the walls, outline included) and their span in the returned picture's px, and k = the held width scaled by returned width / 1024, divided by that span. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- node tools/building-fit.mjs <id>. Its base corners w, s and e give the lower-left foot s.x - w.x and the lower-right foot e.x - s.x: REPORTED, never refused (the slopes of the two feet, 0.50 for a 30 degree camera, go in the record too);

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s, foot to the top of the wall under the eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and the silo are not counted), over the base's width e.x - w.x. Read it on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way (record it as scaffoldReference): within 15 per cent of the scaffold's; r0's number goes in the record too;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall, by eye on a crop;

- highlights (reported, NOT a pass rule: tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them): count opaque pixels with all three channels at or above #ECE4D0, and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on your picture and on r0 (its fitted picture); Art caps the rest at packing;

- by eye: r3 turned, not mirrored (door and loading hatch on the lower-right wall); no door or hatch on r1; the same barn, silo and roof as r0; every surface repainted: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS (crisp courses, planks, timbers or fittings of the same building, never a plain wash); no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall.

A picture refused by `set` for its camera is handled by the tool (GUIDE.md section 7); but a picture that matches the scaffold's silhouette has the game's camera, so if set refuses one that passes the silhouette test, say so in your message with its number and keep the file. A picture that fails the silhouette, the height or the by-eye test is made again from the printed prompt: keep the view's paint-over paragraph and add ONE more paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the roof ridge 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a plank wall is wanted"; it replaces the added one from the attempt before. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours; never attach a picture that failed; never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 6 calls in all. If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, run set <id> rejected --note "<the test it failed and its number>" (the tool sets the file aside), and say so in your message; never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop after that view and say so.

CLOSE. Leave r0 and r2 as they are. If the new r1 or r3 shows walls the old r2 does not copy (the back from r1, the left-hand side from r3), say so in your message, and do not repaint r2: that is the author's call. node tools/building-check.mjs --family farm; node tools/building-sheets.mjs --family farm, then look at review/farm.png. Commit "Art: small farm halt, r1 and r3 painted over the scaffolds", push the branch and stop. In your message give the review sheet's path and, for each attempt: the view, its base span (px of the returned picture), k and the held span, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the feet slopes, the height number against the scaffold's, the highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which pictures passed every test.

PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. Its surfaces are soft, smeared or patchy, and every wall that r0 did not show is INVENTED by the 3D reconstruction (the invented surfaces of each view are named below under THE VIEWS). Painting stays painting: every call is an image EDIT of the lit render in which you repaint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), matching r0's materials and details wherever r0 shows them. The invented surfaces are painted anew: plausibly, in the same building's style, from the materials and details of the sides r0 does show, and never left soft, blurred, smeared or plain. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening and part stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline, below).

THE MASTER. assets/source/buildings-v2/fuel_stop/fuel_stop-a0-r0.png, sha256 49f701ce3e2bd4a3c4b0a2a6194fd1470a56390dda9f241dd7a82c9a2e6c6ce4. Check the hash first; if it differs, stop and say so. The right-hand side (lower-right in r0) is the LONG side, three piers and two braced bays.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The stage's pier span, from the outer edge of its leftmost pier's foot to the outer edge of its rightmost pier's foot, is held at 516 px of the canvas (632 px of a 1254 px wide picture): Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its piers' span is that. A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted span no longer refuses a picture: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize it yourself. Record the span and k = held span / measured span.

MEASURE BEFORE YOU RECORD (after take, before set), in this order. There is NO separate camera test, foot-ratio test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

- silhouette, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (../scaffolds-535/fuel_stop-r<v>.json: canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 = returned width / 1024 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute (a) the overlap IoU (intersection over union of the two filled shapes) at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture, chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (symmetric Hausdorff), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask so that antialiasing specks do not count. PASS needs an IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px). Write the small script yourself (it reads pictureOf from tools/building-check.mjs for the alpha; it is not committed unless the folder already has a measuring script for this family); a picture that fails is repainted, never nudged, stretched or reshaped to fit;

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The pier span is held at 516 px of the canvas (632 of 1254): on a crop, read the leftmost pier's foot and the rightmost pier's foot (outer edges; chute and chain do not count), and k = held span scaled by returned width / 1024, divided by that span. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- node tools/building-fit.mjs <id>. Its base corners w, s and e give the lower-left foot s.x - w.x and the lower-right foot e.x - s.x: REPORTED, never refused (the slopes of the two feet, 0.50 for a 30 degree camera, go in the record too);

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): (s.y - box.top) / (e.x - w.x), with the base corners s, e and w that node tools/building-fit.mjs prints for the picture as it was returned (the pier span e.x - w.x) and box.top its opaque box's top row (r0 reads 1.531: s.y 1128, box.top 154, span 636 of 1254; r2 reads 1.484). Read it on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way (record it as scaffoldReference): within 15 per cent of the scaffold's; r0's number goes in the record too;

- light: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall, by eye on a crop;

- highlights (reported, NOT a pass rule: tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them): count opaque pixels with all three channels at or above #ECE4D0, and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on your picture and on r0 (its fitted picture); Art caps the rest at packing;

- by eye: r3 turned, not mirrored (winch, chain and chute at the lower-right wall's near end, inside the opening); no chute, winch or chain on a hidden wall; six piers, three along the long side; the same bunker, coal, straps and legs as r0 and the accepted r2; every surface repainted: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces named under THE VIEWS (crisp courses, planks, timbers or fittings of the same building, never a plain wash); no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object painted; the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall.

A picture refused by `set` for its camera is handled by the tool (GUIDE.md section 7); but a picture that matches the scaffold's silhouette has the game's camera, so if set refuses one that passes the silhouette test, say so in your message with its number and keep the file. A picture that fails the silhouette, the height or the by-eye test is made again from the printed prompt: keep the view's paint-over paragraph and add ONE more paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1 left the roof ridge 31 canvas px above the scaffold's outline, and the back wall soft where a plank wall is wanted"; it replaces the added one from the attempt before. Always edit the lit render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours; never attach a picture that failed; never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 6 calls in all. If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, run set <id> rejected --note "<the test it failed and its number>" (the tool sets the file aside), and say so in your message; never accept it quietly. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, stop after that view and say so.

CLOSE THE FAMILY. node tools/building-check.mjs --family fuel_stop; node tools/building-sheets.mjs --family fuel_stop, then look at review/fuel_stop.png: all four views should be one stage of one height, r1 and r3 matching r2. Commit "Art: fuel_stop, r1 and r3 painted over the scaffolds, matching the accepted r2", push the branch and stop. PAINTING OVER A SCAFFOLD (the author's decision of 19 October 2026). The lit render is a rough 3D picture: its shape, its camera and its layout are right, and everything else about it is not a painting. THE BUILDING (r0). A red-brick pump house on cream limestone quoins and base. Its front is the long side: two bays, the door bay (forest-green plank door, lamp over it, a small slate-roofed porch gable a little forward of the wall), a downpipe, a plain bay, and a big quoined pier at the end. The short side and the back are plain brick between quoins: no door, no window, no porch, no lamp. On the house a dark steel frame with X bracing, and on it a riveted forest-green steel tank with two oat-cream bands, a railing, a manhole and a vent, as long as the long side and as deep as the house, covering the whole roof. At the end of the front next to the left-hand side wall stands a tall forest-green hose crane: ONE post with one diagonal brace and ONE jib about 0.43 tile long (the width of the short side), the hose hanging from the jib's end. Pipes with copper bands run from the tank to the crane. THE LIGHT never turns with the building. It comes from the upper left: tops lightest, the lower-left wall mid (r0's front, luma 112), the lower-right wall darkest (r0's short side, 67). Camera: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, no perspective; both wall feet fall one pixel for every two across.

THE VIEWS, one line each: what the scaffold shows and what it invented. Canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px.

r1 (the front faces the upper left, hidden): INVENTED in the render: the rear wall and the hose. Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (warn: Shape right; the hose arm points away from the camera and its end is a smear. Brick wall invented.)
r3 (the front faces the lower right): INVENTED in the render: the rear wall. Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (good: Door wall seen from the other side and the tank are clean enough; the hose is soft.)
r2 (the front faces the upper right, hidden): INVENTED in the render: both brick walls. Everything else on it is r0's, projected onto the model and soft. The scaffold's own verdict (warn: Both brick walls invented, plain; tank and arm right.)

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 412 px of the canvas (505 px of a 1254 px wide picture). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted width no longer refuses a picture: paint at the size the scaffold has and never resize it yourself. For the Fuel Pump, in every view, measure the base's width from the outer edge of the house's left corner to the outer edge of its right corner at ground level, outline included (the crane's post, the pier and the porch count only where they stand on the ground), and record it with k = held width / measured width.

THE ORDER. r1, then r3, then r2: at most three attempts a view, nine calls in all. Every call is an image EDIT of the lit scaffold render of its view, never of an attempt. References, in this order: r1: (1) ../scaffolds-535/fuel_pump-r1-lit.png, the picture to paint over; (2) docs/art-direction/images/03-theme-town-growth.png, the style board; (3) assets/source/fuel-2026-10-11/refs/fuel_pump-a1-r0-fitted.png, r0 laid onto its footprint. r3: the same three with its own render, and (4) the kept new r1 (only if it passed). r2: (1) its render, (2), (3), and (4) and (5) the kept new r1 and r3 (only if they passed; attach what passed). Never attach more. The bare render and the json are never attached.

The paint-over paragraph. "The first picture is not a grey block-out but a rough 3D render of the Fuel Pump at the game's exact camera: keep its shape, outline, camera and layout exactly as they are (the house, the tank, the frame, the crane's post and jib, the hose, every opening where the render has it, nothing added outside its outline or taken out of it) and paint EVERY surface of it again in the game's painted style: crisp clustered shading, broad masses, three or four shades per material, with r0's brick, quoins, tank bands, steel frame, crane and colours taken from the third picture. The render's surfaces are soft, smeared and patchy, and the walls r0 never showed were invented by the 3D reconstruction (named in the view's paragraph): paint those anew, plausibly and in this building's own brick and quoins, and never leave them soft, blurred, smeared or plain. Light from the upper left. Paint no plinth, ground or shadow. Do not rotate, tilt, resize, shift or reshape the building."

View r1. "The front faces the upper left, hidden: nothing of the front shows on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand side: SHORT, plain brick between limestone quoins with a quoined pier at each end, no door, no window, no porch, no lamp. References paragraph. "References: the first is the scaffold's lit render, the picture to paint over; the second is the style board. Where the fourth (and fifth) reference is a view of this same building, copy its walls at the places this view shares with it: r1's lower-right wall is r2's lower-left wall, r3's lower-left wall is r2's lower-right wall." Output rules: "Output: 1024x1024 RGBA PNG, transparent background, opaque surfaces. Smooth painted contours, broad masses, three or four shades per material. Nothing at or above #ECE4D0 and no yellow or orange as strong as #E8AA48. Never ground, plinth, grass, shadows outside the base, rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow or loose objects."

MEASURE EVERY ATTEMPT, in this order (record-attempt.mjs writes prompts.json; its plan test still holds the old 2.0 to 2.8 and its camera test the old window: take both out, in your own folder, and say so). There is NO separate camera test, plan test or footprint test any more: the scaffold carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides.

- silhouette, the first test. The scaffold's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (../scaffolds-535/fuel_pump-r<v>.json: canvas px, 1024 x 1024), filled and scaled by s0 = returned width / 1024 to the returned picture. The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled (the polygon has none). Compute (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best fit: one uniform factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), which is all Art's packing does to a picture, chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine); record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) the boundary deviation at the best fit: the largest distance, in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f), from any point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and from any point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's (symmetric Hausdorff), after an opening of 2 canvas px on the picture's mask. PASS needs an IoU at the best fit of at least 0.90 AND a boundary deviation of at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; 8 canvas px make a game px);

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture, and k = 505 / that width (scaled by returned width / 1024 if the picture is another size). Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width. The two feet's slopes (0.50 for a 30 degree camera) and the long over short ratio of the house (measure-plan.mjs; read the outer quoin corners by eye where the crane, pipe or pier throws it, and record both) are REPORTED, never refused;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the brick wall at the near corner, foot to the top of its quoins, over the base's width e.x - w.x, read on the picture and on the same view's bare scaffold render the same way (scaffoldReference): within 15 per cent of the scaffold's; r0's number (0.43) goes in the record. Not lower than r0's;

- light: light-check.mjs with a box on plain brick of the lower-left wall, then one of the lower-right wall, clear of quoins and the crane: the lower-right median at most 0.75 times the lower-left's, the lower-left between 100 and 150 and the lower-right between 60 and 85;

- crane: exactly one post and one jib, no second post and no beam over the tank; the post's top above the tank's top; the jib's reach 0.43 tile, about 92 px of the canvas (75 to 110 px); the hose ending at the door's top in r3;

- by eye, set beside r0 on grass as in review/: it is the same building, the plain walls plain, no door, porch or lamp on a hidden front, the crane at the corner the paragraph says; every surface repainted: no soft, blurred, smeared or patchy surface of the render is left anywhere, and above all not on the invented surfaces (crisp brick courses and quoins, never a plain wash).

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: repaint from the lit render again with the same attachments (never attach a failed picture) and add exactly ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's jib ended 38 canvas px outside the scaffold's outline at the upper left, and the rear wall was soft"; it replaces the earlier Correction, so that no prompt has more than one. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture: the shape comes from painting over the scaffold, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. If none of a view's three attempts passes, keep the closest in raw/, say which test failed with its number, copy nothing over the view's file, and go on to the next view. If the silhouette's IoU is under 0.80 in all three attempts of a view, say so and stop that view.

KEEPING. A picture that passes every test is copied over fuel_pump-a1-r<v>.png (r1 replaces attempt 12). Records: add every attempt to prompts.json "attempts" as {view, attempt, selected, file, prompt, references, returned, measured} (record-attempt.mjs does it), with the silhouette numbers, the width and k, the height and scaffoldReference, the light values with their boxes, the crane's numbers, and what you saw. Update "selection" and "qa". Put a grass sheet of each kept picture in review/. Commit after each view: "Art: Fuel Pump r1 painted over the scaffold" and the like, and push.

END. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the boundary deviation), the base's width, the scale k and the held width, the slopes of the feet, the long over short ratio, the height against the scaffold's, the light values, the crane's numbers, and which attempt was kept and why; and say plainly whether r1, r3 and r2 passed.

"Edit this grey block-out into ONE finished stone tower windmill WITHOUT SAILS, for a pastoral
isometric railway game. Style: the attached style board; crisp clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material. Materials as the third and fourth pictures: cream
limestone courses with a few grey stones, a dark slate cone cap, an evergreen plank door with
iron straps. Light from the upper left: the cap's left side and the tower's upper left lightest,
the lower-left face mid-tone, the lower-right face darkest. Nothing brighter than a soft cream: no
pure-white highlights, nothing orange.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera and turn exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees
down. The tower is round and smooth, no facets: its foot is an ellipse half as tall as it is
wide, and every stone course curves like that foot (down in the middle of the face), not as a
straight line. Keep the tower's size, taper and place, and the cone cap's height: the cone's tip
stands where the block-out's does, with a plain tip, no finial, no ball. The dark door stays on
the LOWER-LEFT face where the block-out has it, at ground level on the tower's foot, as tall as the
block-out's door (about a quarter of the tower's foot width), an evergreen plank door with iron
straps and a plain stone frame; no step, no sack, nothing in front. One narrow window may sit
above it on the same face, on the same centre line. The windshaft: keep the block-out's stub, a
short round shaft of dark iron-banded timber coming out of the cap's lower-left slope and pointing
down and to the left along the lower-left face, two pixels across for each pixel down, and
ending in the block-out's dark iron hub plate, the same oval at the same place; the plate is bare,
with nothing on it.

Do not paint the grey slab: the tower stands straight on the ground, its foot plain and clean. No
sails, no stocks, no timber lattice, no square iron plate, no shadow, ground, grass, sacks, people,
text, border or glow. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha 0) and an opaque
building. References: the first is the block-out to edit; the second the style board; the third
the game's present windmill, for stone, slate and door only (not its sails); the fourth an earlier
body, for stone, slate and window only, drawn from a camera that is wrong: do not copy its angle."

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU GO ON. Write check-body.mjs once in the folder; it reads the
picture and prints each number below with PASS or FAIL. Only the foot is read by machine:
- camera: from tools/building-fit.mjs, roundCamera(png) (ratio, and degrees off), and from
  tools/building-check.mjs, checkFile(path, 't1', 0, { round: true }), which must list no
  problem (background, edge, shadow). Do not run it through the command line form: that writes
  assets/source/buildings-v2/report.json, which is not yours. The foot columns are those whose
  lowest pixel is within 0.4 of the building's width of the lowest pixel of all; W is their span,
  xc their middle, yc = lowest pixel - 0.25 W (the ellipse's centre), s = W / 42.99 picture px per
  game px.
Everything else you read off a zoomed crop of the picture and pass to the script as coordinates.
A picture passes when:
- foot ellipse: minor over major 0.50 +-0.04, at most 2 degrees off (a picture 2 to 3 degrees
  off that passes the rest is a near keeper: kept and reported; if the best attempt is still more
  than 3 degrees off, stop, do not paint again);
- tower: (yc - the topmost opaque pixel) / W within 5% of the block-out's 1.105 (1.05 to 1.16);
  W over the shorter side of the picture is recorded, not a test (Art scales a returned picture uniformly at packing, so the tower's size in the picture does not refuse it);
- stub: two points on the top edge of the stub, one near the cap, one near the plate; the edge
  goes down to the left at slope 0.50 +-0.08;
- hub: the plate's centre, ((x - xc) / s, (y - yc) / s - 4), is within 2 game px of (-16, -34);
- door: its centre column is (x - xc) / W = -0.354 +-0.04; its height over W is 0.251 +-0.03
  (a 2.5 m door reads 0.30);
- clean: pixels with hue 8 to 48 degrees, saturation 0.45 or more and value 0.60 or less (the sail
  timber; the stone's own warmth is not counted) outside the door and the hub plate: at most 25;
  opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0: 0; within 12
  (RGB distance) of selection amber #E8AA48: 0. A picture whose only miss is the near-white count
  is a keeper all the same: record the count, and Art caps those pixels when it packs the sprite;
  name it in a Correction only while another test also failed;
- by eye, on your own look at the zoomed picture: a round smooth tower, no sails or stocks or
  lattice, no plinth, no finial, nothing standing at the foot, nothing orange, the lower-left face
  lighter than the lower-right, the door and the shaft on the lower-left face, the cap slate.

A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole, add under it one paragraph beginning "Correction:"

C17c's kept body, the same tower seen from the front: take its stone courses, its slate cap and its
colours, nothing of its door, its window or its stub, which are on a different side of the
building. Ask for 1024 x 1024, transparent background. Do not fix a returned picture by resizing,
cropping, flipping, shearing or repainting it, and never build a view from another view's pixels:
each view is turned by its own camera, painted on its own guide.

THE PROMPT, word for word, every attempt: the shared text, then the view's paragraph, then the
closing paragraph (then any Correction paragraphs below it).

Shared text:
"Edit this grey block-out into ONE finished stone tower windmill WITHOUT SAILS, for a pastoral
isometric railway game. Style: the attached style board; crisp clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material. Materials as the third and fourth pictures: cream
limestone courses with a few grey stones, a dark slate cone cap. Light from the upper left, and it
does not turn with the building: the cap's left side and the tower's upper left lightest, the
lower-left face mid-tone, the lower-right face darkest. Nothing brighter than a soft cream: no
pure-white highlights, nothing orange.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera and turn exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees
down. The tower is round and smooth, no facets: its foot is an ellipse half as tall as it is
wide, and every stone course curves like that foot (down in the middle of the face), not as a
straight line. Keep the tower's size, taper and place, and the cone cap's height: the cone's tip
stands where the block-out's does, with a plain tip, no finial, no ball. The windshaft: keep the
block-out's stub, a short round shaft of dark iron-banded timber coming out of the cap and
ending in the block-out's dark iron hub plate, the same oval at the same place and of the same
size; the plate is bare, with nothing on it, and nothing hides its middle."

View r3 paragraph:
"This is the view from the lower right. The stub comes out of the cap's lower-right slope and
points down and to the right along the lower-right face, one pixel down for two across, in front
of the tower, and ends in the plate at the cap's right edge. The dark door is on the LOWER-RIGHT
face where the block-out has it, at ground level on the tower's foot, as tall as the block-out's
door (about a quarter of the tower's foot width): an evergreen plank door with iron straps and a
plain stone frame, no step, no sack, nothing in front. One narrow window may sit above it on the
same centre line."

View r1 paragraph:
"This is the view from behind the windmill. The front is away from the camera: there is no door
and no window on either visible face, only plain stone courses; the lower-left face is lighter than
the lower-right. The windshaft stub stands BEHIND the tower: it comes out of the far side of the
cap, its root hidden by the cap, and points up and to the left, one pixel up for two across, and
ends in the plate above the cap's left shoulder, in the open, not hidden by the cap."

View r2 paragraph:
"This is the view from behind the windmill, turned a quarter further. The front is away from the
camera: there is no door and no window on either visible face, only plain stone courses; the
lower-left face is lighter than the lower-right. The windshaft stub stands BEHIND the tower: it
comes out of the far side of the cap, its root hidden by the cap, and points up and to the right,
one pixel up for two across, and ends in the plate above the cap's right shoulder, in the open,
not hidden by the cap."

Closing paragraph:
"Do not paint the grey slab: the tower stands straight on the ground, its foot plain and clean. No
sails, no stocks, no timber lattice, no square iron plate, no shadow, ground, grass, sacks, people,
text, border or glow. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha 0) and an opaque
building. References: the first is the block-out to edit; the second the style board; the third
the game's present windmill, for stone, slate and door only (not its sails); the fourth the same
tower seen from the front, for stone, slate and colours only: it has a door, a window and a
stub on another side, which do not belong to this view."

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU GO ON. C17c wrote check-body.mjs in this folder; give it the view
(1, 2 or 3) and the table's hub and stub direction for it, and have it print each number below
with PASS or FAIL. Only the foot is read by machine:
- camera: from tools/building-fit.mjs, roundCamera(png) (ratio, and degrees off), and from
  tools/building-check.mjs, checkFile(path, 't1', 0, { round: true }), which must list no
  problem. Do not run it through the command line form: that writes
  assets/source/buildings-v2/report.json, which is not yours. The foot columns are those whose
  lowest pixel is within 0.4 of the building's width of the lowest pixel of all; W is their span,
  xc their middle, yc = lowest pixel - 0.25 W (the ellipse's centre), s = W / 42.99 picture px per
  game px.
Everything else you read off a zoomed crop and pass to the script as coordinates. A picture passes
when:
- foot ellipse: minor over major 0.50 +-0.04, at most 2 degrees off (a picture 2 to 3 degrees
  off that passes the rest is a near keeper: kept and reported; if the best attempt of the view is
  still more than 3 degrees off, stop that view, do not paint it again);
- the tower stands as the guide's: xc within 12 px of 512 and yc within 12 px of 800 (scale these
  by the returned width / 1024), and W recorded against the W in hub.json of r0 (a measurement, not a test: Art scales each returned picture uniformly at packing, by the ratio of the two, so that the four views are one windmill at one scale);
- tower: (yc - the cone's tip) / W within 5% of 1.105 (1.05 to 1.16). The tip is the cone's own
  point, read on the crop: in r1 and r2 the plate stands higher than the tip, so the topmost
  opaque pixel is the plate, not the tip;
- stub: two points on the top edge of the stub, one near the cap or its edge, one near the plate,
  at least 40 px apart; the edge runs at slope 0.50 +-0.08 and in the view's direction (up to the
  left in r1, up to the right in r2, down to the right in r3);
- hub: the plate's centre, ((x - xc) / s, (y - yc) / s - 4), is within 2 game px of the view's
  hub: (-16, -50) in r1, (16, -50) in r2, (16, -34) in r3. The plate must be seen whole, as in the
  guide; in r1 and r2 nothing of the cap covers its middle;
- door: r3 only: its centre column is (x - xc) / W = +0.354 +-0.04; its height over W is 0.251
  +-0.03 (a 2.5 m door reads 0.30). In r1 and r2: no door, no window, nothing dark and
  door-shaped on the face;
- clean: pixels with hue 8 to 48 degrees, saturation 0.45 or more and value 0.60 or less (the sail
  timber; the stone's own warmth is not counted) outside the door, the stub and the hub plate: at
  most 25; opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0: 0;
  within 12 (RGB distance) of selection amber #E8AA48: 0. A picture whose only miss is the
  near-white count is a keeper all the same: record the count, and Art caps those pixels when it
  packs the sprite; name it in a Correction only while another test also failed;
- by eye, on your own look at the zoomed picture: a round smooth tower, no sails or stocks or
  lattice, no plinth, no finial, nothing standing at the foot, nothing orange, the lower-left face
  lighter than the lower-right, the cap slate, and, in r1 and r2, the stub and plate behind the
  tower with nothing in front of the tower where a door would be.

A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole, add under it one paragraph beginning "Correction:"
that names the number it missed, as "your last r1 body's plate stood at (-16, -41) where the
block-out's is (-16, -50)", and keep every earlier Correction paragraph of that view. Always edit
the guide, never an attempt. At most three attempts a view. If none passes, keep the closest in
raw/, copy it to the kept file all the same, mark it kept: false with the test it failed in
prompts.json, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly.

THE PICTURE PROMPT. Send this to the image tool as it stands, with the pose line of the pose in place of <POSE>. Ask for 1024 x 1536.
"EDIT IMAGE1, a grey pose mannequin, into a finished painted game sprite of the man in IMAGE2. Return exactly 1024 x 1536 pixels, real transparent RGBA. Keep everything IMAGE1 says about the pose and the camera: the size of the figure, where the head, trunk, arms, legs and boots are, the lean, the 45 degree turn and the 2:1 isometric camera 30 degrees down. The legs are the grey legs: paint each trouser thigh, knee, shin and boot over the grey one, in the same place and at the same angles; do not re-pose, straighten, mirror, recentre, enlarge or shrink anything. He walks toward the upper right of the picture; we see his back and his right side in three quarters, never a face from the front. <POSE> IMAGE2 shows only his upper body, cut off at the hips and at half the size: take from it his head, hair, beard, moss-green flat cap, rust-orange and forest-green checked flannel shirt with rolled sleeves, skin and build, as a painting at the guide's size. Paint his legs yourself: charcoal-grey work trousers with turned-up cuffs, dark brown leather boots. The grey bar on his right shoulder in IMAGE1 is his axe: paint it as his wood-handled felling axe resting on his right shoulder, held by his right hand where the guide's hand is, the head behind his shoulder; his left arm swings as the guide's does. No other tool. Style: painted, broad smooth clusters of three or four shades per material, one light from the upper left, dark only on the lower and side rims, no outline round the whole figure, muted natural colours; nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48. Alpha 0 background and an opaque figure; no ground, floor, shadow, glow, haze, text, border or extra object."
POSE lines. P1: "Pose 1 of 4, contact: his right leg, on the picture's right, leads with the heel down and the toe up; his left leg trails on the picture's left, toe down and heel lifted; left arm forward, right arm back." P2: "Pose 2 of 4, passing: his right leg is planted flat and standing; his left leg swings, thigh forward, knee bent, the foot clear of the ground; both arms near the body." P3: "Pose 3 of 4, contact: his left leg leads with the heel down and the toe up; his right leg trails, toe down and heel lifted; right arm forward, left arm back." P4: "Pose 4 of 4, passing: his left leg is planted flat and standing; his right leg swings, thigh forward, knee bent, the foot clear of the ground; both arms near the body."

THE TOOL FOR THE NUMBERS. v3/measure-v3.mjs, v3/pose-png.mjs and v3/contact-overlap.mjs already exist from the earlier runs, and measure-v3.mjs reproduces the calibration below: use them from v3/ instead of writing and copying (run the guides first, as asked below; if a number differs, fix a copy in v3/run-3/, never the original). THE TOOL FOR THE NUMBERS. Write v3/measure-v3.mjs. Copy people-2026-10-11/pose-png.mjs and contact-overlap.mjs into v3/ (their paths then stay in v3/); take isOverWhite and isAmberStrong from tools/atlas-cap.mjs, and the guide joints and groundPoint from assets/source/people-v3-guides/manifest.json (guides entry back-shoulder-p<n>.png). Measure on the picture as returned; if it is another 2:3 size, scale columns and rows by width / 1024 first; any other shape fails. "Opaque" is alpha above 128. Tests per picture (guide numbers in brackets, P1, P2, P3, P4):
1. Height: box() height of the picture within 3% of the same on the guide (measure each back-shoulder guide with your tool; the axe may stand above the head, so measure the figure without anything above the cap's top).
2. Soles: the lowest opaque row within 8 px of row 1440, the guide's (a quarter of a texel at the game's zoom 4).
3. Planted foot. For a foot (right at P1, P2, P3; left at P3, P4) take the opaque pixels inside the window x from the least to the most of that foot's heel, toe and ankle canvas x in the manifest, minus and plus 30; y from 145 above the lowest of those joints' y, to 30 below it. Let ylow be the lowest opaque row in the window; the foot's point (cx, cy) is the mean of the opaque pixels of the window in rows ylow - 40 to ylow; f = ((cx - 512) - 2 * (cy - gy)) / 1717.6, gy the guide's groundPoint row. Slide of a pair of poses (1,2), (2,3), (3,4) is f of the earlier minus f of the later, on the same foot. The back-swing guides give f 0.1106, -0.0068, -0.0403 (right, P1 to P3) and 0.0769, 0.0148 (left, P3, P4), slides 0.1173, 0.0335, 0.0621; the back-shoulder guides have the same legs, so your tool should give the same on them; use the values it gives on the back-shoulder guides. The gait's step is 0.123 of the height per pose; the test is that each slide of the picture is within 0.01 of the guide's. A pair is checked when its later pose is painted, against the kept earlier picture; a miss belongs to the later pose.
4. Repeat: contactOverlap() of the kept P1 with P3 below 0.45 (hatTop and soleRow taken from each picture's box); the back-swing guides give 0.221. Report the P2 with P4 figure too (the guide's is 0.504); it is not a pass rule.
5. Turned: the topmost opaque row in columns 352 to 412 (L) and in columns 614 to 668 (R); (R - L) / 960 within 0.03 of the same measure on the back-shoulder guide of the pose (the back-swing guides give 0.070; a figure straight away from the camera gives under 0); on a painted figure the axe's head and the cap lie in those columns, so their top row is not the shoulder: report the machine reading, but make the test by eye. On a zoomed crop of the picture beside its guide read the row of the top of the picture-left shoulder (L) and of the picture-right shoulder (R), where the shoulder's line meets the neck and the sleeve, under the axe's head and the cap; record both numbers and the same two read on the back-shoulder guide; the test is (R - L) / 960 within 0.03 of the guide's reading. A reading you cannot make because the axe covers both shoulders is not a stop: say so, and judge the turn by eye (the back of the head and the right side, no face from the front). By eye also: the back of the head and the right side, no face from the front.
6. Caps (a REPORT, not a pass rule, and never a reason to stop): record the number of pixels with alpha 16 or more for which isOverWhite is true, the number for which isAmberStrong is true, and the number of pixels with alpha 1 to 15 (haze). tools/atlas-cap.mjs (issue #465) scales both colours down and zeroes alpha below 16 at packing, which is why the earlier runs' 3 to 49 near-white and up to 1616 amber pixels are not a reason to stop. If either count is over 300, add one plain sentence to the next Correction ("your shirt's orange came out as strong as the selection amber"), and go on.
7. Real RGBA: PNG colour type 6, all four corners alpha 0.
Before any call, run the tool on the four back-swing guides and check it gives the numbers above (they are Art's, from the same code); if it does not, fix your tool, or if the words are ambiguous stop and say what differs. Then run it on the four back-shoulder guides and record their numbers in prompts.json: they are the targets. Make it print every number for every picture.

PROCEDURE. Paint P1, P2, P3, P4 in that order, one call each, then measure. A picture is accepted only when tests 1 to 5 and 7 pass (test 6 is a report). After a failed attempt, send the same prompt whole, from the guide and the identity picture again (never from the failed picture), with one added paragraph beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed in plain words ("your last picture's lowest sole was on row 1411, the guide's is 1440", "the shoulders were level, the guide's right shoulder stands 67 px lower than the left"). At most three attempts a pose and twelve calls in all. If the same test fails on the same pose twice, stop there and report; the guide is then to be changed, not the prompt. If a pose uses its three attempts without passing, keep the closest, record the failed test, stop and say so. Do not paint on after a failed pose.

Commit ("Art: lumberjack-back-walk repainted whole on the pose guides") and push the branch. If a pose failed, still commit the raw pictures, prompts.json and the tool as "Art: R7c-1 trial attempts recorded", and push.
